</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Exceeding 80 mm in diameter or length of major axis and 20 mm in thickness (depth);</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>"Space-qualified" components for optical systems, as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Components lightweighted to less than 20 % "equivalent density" compared with a solid blank of the same aperture and thickness;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Raw substrates, processed substrates having surface coatings (single-layer or multi-layer, metallic or dielectric, conducting, semiconducting or insulating) or having protective films;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>Segments or assemblies of mirrors designed to be assembled in space into an optical system with a collecting aperture equivalent to or larger than a single optic 1 m in diameter;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>4.</td><td>Components manufactured from "composite" materials having a coefficient of linear thermal expansion, in any coordinate direction, equal to or less than 5 × 10-6/K;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>d.</td><td>Optical control equipment as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Equipment specially designed to maintain the surface figure or orientation of the "space-qualified" components specified in 6A004.c.1. or 6A004.c.3.;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Steering, tracking, stabilisation and resonator alignment equipment as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>Beam steering mirror stages designed to carry mirrors having diameter or major axis length greater than 50 mm and having all of the following, and specially designed electronic control equipment therefor:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>A maximum angular travel of ±26 mrad or more;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>A mechanical resonant frequency of 500 Hz or more; and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>An angular "accuracy" of 10 μrad (microradians) or less (better);</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Resonator alignment equipment having bandwidths equal to or more than 100 Hz and an "accuracy" of 10 μrad or less (better);</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>Gimbals having all of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>A maximum slew exceeding 5°;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>A bandwidth of 100 Hz or more;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>Angular pointing errors of 200 μrad (microradians) or less; and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>d.</td><td>Having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Exceeding 0,15 m but not exceeding 1 m in diameter or major axis length and capable of angular accelerations exceeding 2 rad (radians)/s2; or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Exceeding 1 m in diameter or major axis length and capable of angular accelerations exceeding 0,5 rad (radians)/s2;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>4.</td><td>Not used;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>e.</td><td>'Aspheric optical elements' having all of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Largest dimension of the optical-aperture greater than 400 mm;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Surface roughness less than 1 nm (rms) for sampling lengths equal to or greater than 1 mm; and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>Coefficient of linear thermal expansion's absolute magnitude less than 3 × 10-6/K at 25 °C.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Notes: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>For the purposes of 6A004.e., an 'aspheric optical element' is any element used in an optical system whose imaging surface or surfaces are designed to depart from the shape of an ideal sphere.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>For the purposes of 6A004.e.2., manufacturers are not required to measure the surface roughness unless the optical element was designed or manufactured with the intent to meet, or exceed, the control parameter.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>Note</td><td>6A004.e. does not control 'aspheric optical elements' having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>Largest optical-aperture dimension less than 1 m and focal length to aperture ratio equal to or greater than 4,5:1;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Largest optical-aperture dimension equal to or greater than 1 m and focal length to aperture ratio equal to or greater than 7:1;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>Designed as Fresnel, flyeye, stripe, prism or diffractive optical elements;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>d.</td><td>Fabricated from borosilicate glass having a coefficient of linear thermal expansion greater than 2,5 × 10-6/K at 25 °C;or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>e.</td><td>An X-ray optical element having inner mirror capabilities (e.g., tube-type mirrors).</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>For 'aspheric optical elements' specially designed for lithography equipment, see 3B001.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>f.</td><td>Dynamic wavefront measuring equipment having all of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>'Frame rates' equal to or more than 1 kHz; and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>A wavefront accuracy equal to or less (better) than λ/20 at the designed wavelength.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Note:  For the purposes of 6A004.f., 'frame rate' is a frequency at which all "active pixels" in the "focal plane array" are integrated for recording images projected by the wavefront sensor optics. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>6A005</td><td>"Lasers", other than those specified in 0B001.g.5. or 0B001.h.6., components and optical equipment, as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>SEE ALSO 6A205.</td></tr>

</tbody>
</table>

 Technical Notes:  For the purposes of 6A005.d.1.: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Semiconductor "lasers" are commonly called "laser" diodes.</td></tr>

<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>A 'bar' (also called a semiconductor "laser" 'bar', a "laser" diode 'bar' or diode 'bar') consists of multiple semiconductor "lasers" in a one-dimensional array.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>6A006</td><td>"Magnetometers", "magnetic gradiometers", "intrinsic magnetic gradiometers", underwater electric field sensors, "compensation systems", and specially designed components therefor, as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>SEE ALSO 7A103.d.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>"Magnetometers" and subsystems as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>"Magnetometers" using "superconductive" (SQUID) "technology" and having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>SQUID systems designed for stationary operation, without specially designed subsystems designed to reduce in-motion noise, and having a 'sensitivity' equal to or less (better) than 50 fT (rms) per square root Hz at a frequency of 1 Hz; or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>SQUID systems having an in-motion-magnetometer 'sensitivity' less (better) than 20 pT (rms) per square root Hz at a frequency of 1 Hz and specially designed to reduce in-motion noise;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>"Magnetometers" using optically pumped or nuclear precession (proton/Overhauser) "technology" having a 'sensitivity' less (better) than 20 pT (rms) per square root Hz at a frequency of 1 Hz;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>"Magnetometers" using fluxgate "technology" having a 'sensitivity' equal to or less (better) than 10 pT (rms) per square root Hz at a frequency of 1 Hz;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>4.</td><td>Induction coil "magnetometers" having a 'sensitivity' less (better) than any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>0,05 nT (rms) per square root Hz at frequencies of less than 1 Hz;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>1 × 10-3 nT (rms) per square root Hz at frequencies of 1 Hz or more but not exceeding 10 Hz; or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>1 × 10-4 nT (rms) per square root Hz at frequencies exceeding 10 Hz;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>5.</td><td>Fibre optic "magnetometers" having a 'sensitivity' less (better) than 1 nT (rms) per square root Hz;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Underwater electric field sensors having a 'sensitivity' less (better) than 8 nanovolt per metre per square root Hz when measured at 1 Hz;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>"Magnetic gradiometers" as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>"Magnetic gradiometers" using multiple "magnetometers" specified in 6A006.a.;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Fibre optic "intrinsic magnetic gradiometers" having a magnetic gradient field 'sensitivity' less (better) than 0,3 nT/m rms per square root Hz;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>"Intrinsic magnetic gradiometers", using "technology" other than fibre-optic "technology", having a magnetic gradient field 'sensitivity' less (better) than 0,015 nT/m rms per square root Hz;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>d.</td><td>"Compensation systems" for magnetic or underwater electric field sensors resulting in a performance equal to or better than the specified parameters of 6A006.a., 6A006.b. or 6A006.c.;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>e.</td><td>Underwater electromagnetic receivers incorporating magnetic field sensors specified in 6A006.a. or underwater electric field sensors specified in 6A006.b.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Note:  For the purposes of 6A006, 'sensitivity' (noise level) is the root mean square of the device-limited noise floor which is the lowest signal that can be measured. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>6A007</td><td>Gravity meters (gravimeters) and gravity gradiometers, as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>SEE ALSO 6A107.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>Gravity meters designed or modified for ground use and having a static "accuracy" of less (better) than 10 μGal;<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>6A007.a. does not control ground gravity meters of the quartz element (Worden) type.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Gravity meters designed for mobile platforms and having all of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>A static "accuracy" of less (better) than 0,7 mGal; and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>An in-service (operational) "accuracy" of less (better) than 0,7 mGal having a "time-to-steady-state registration" of less than 2 minutes under any combination of attendant corrective compensations and motional influences;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>Gravity gradiometers.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>6A008</td><td>Radar systems, equipment and assemblies, having any of the following, and specially designed components therefor:<table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>SEE ALSO 6A108.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>6A008 does not control:<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Secondary surveillance radar (SSR);</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Civil Automotive Radar;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Displays or monitors used for air traffic control (ATC);</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Meteorological (weather) radar;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Precision approach radar (PAR) equipment conforming to ICAO standards and employing electronically steerable linear (1-dimensional) arrays or mechanically positioned passive antennae.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>Operating at frequencies from 40 GHz to 230 GHz and having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>An average output power exceeding 100 mW; or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Locating "accuracy" of 1 m or less (better) in range and 0,2 degree or less (better) in azimuth;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>A “tunable” bandwidth exceeding ± 6,25 % of the 'centre operating frequency'; Technical Note:  For the purposes of 6A008.b., the 'centre operating frequency' equals one half of the sum of the highest plus the lowest specified operating frequencies. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>Capable of operating simultaneously on more than two carrier frequencies;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>d.</td><td>Capable of operating in synthetic aperture (SAR), inverse synthetic aperture (ISAR) radar mode, or sidelooking airborne (SLAR) radar mode;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>e.</td><td>Incorporating electronically scanned array antennae; Technical Note:  For the purposes of 6A008.e., electronically scanned array antennae are also known as electronically steerable array antennae. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>f.</td><td>Capable of heightfinding non-cooperative targets;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>g.</td><td>Specially designed for airborne (balloon or airframe mounted) operation and having Doppler "signal processing" for the detection of moving targets;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>h.</td><td>Employing processing of radar signals and using any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>"Radar spread spectrum" techniques; or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>'Radar frequency agility' techniques;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Note:  For the purposes of 6A008.h., 'radar frequency agility' applies to any technique which changes, in a pseudo-random sequence, the carrier frequency of a pulsed radar transmitter between pulses or between groups of pulses by an amount equal to or larger than the pulse bandwidth. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>i.</td><td>Providing ground-based operation with a maximum 'instrumented range' exceeding 185 km;<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>6A008.i. does not control:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>Fishing ground surveillance radar;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Ground radar equipment specially designed for enroute air traffic control and having all the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>A maximum 'instrumented range' of 500 km or less;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Configured so that radar target data can be transmitted only one way from the radar site to one or more civil ATC centres;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>Contains no provisions for remote control of the radar scan rate from the enroute ATC centre;and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>4.</td><td>Permanently installed;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>Weather balloon tracking radars.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Note:  For the purposes of 6A008.i. 'instrumented range' is the specified unambiguous display range of a radar. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>j.</td><td>Being "laser" radar or Light Detection and Ranging (LIDAR) equipment and having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>"Space-qualified";</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Employing coherent heterodyne or homodyne detection techniques and having an angular resolution of less (better) than 20 μrad (microradians); or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>Designed for carrying out airborne bathymetric littoral surveys to International Hydrographic Organization (IHO) Order 1a Standard (5th Edition February 2008) for Hydrographic Surveys or better, and using one or more "lasers" with a wavelength exceeding 400 nm but not exceeding 600 nm;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>Note 1:</td><td>LIDAR equipment specially designed for surveying is only specified in 6A008.j.3.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>Note 2:</td><td>6A008.j. does not control LIDAR equipment specially designed for meteorological observation.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>Note 3:</td><td>Parameters in the IHO Order 1a Standard (5th Edition February 2008) are summarised as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Horizontal Accuracy (95 % Confidence Level) = 5 m + 5 % of depth.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Depth Accuracy for Reduced Depths (95 % confidence level) = ±√(a2+(b × d)2), where:<table><tbody><tr><td>a = 0,5 m = constant depth error,</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>i.e., the sum of all constant depth errors</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b = 0,013 = factor of depth dependent error</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b × d = depth dependent error,</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>i.e., the sum of all depth dependent errors d = depth</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Feature Detection = Cubic features &gt; 2 m in depths up to 40 m; 10 % of depth beyond 40 m.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>k.</td><td>Having "signal processing" sub-systems using "pulse compression" and having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>A "pulse compression" ratio exceeding 150; or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>A compressed pulse width of less than 200 ns; or<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>6A008.k.2. does not control two dimensional 'marine radar' or 'vessel traffic service' radar, having all of the following;<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td> "Pulse compression" ratio not exceeding 150;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Compressed pulse width of greater than 30 ns;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>Single and rotating mechanically scanned antenna;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>d.</td><td>Peak output power not exceeding 250 W;and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>e.</td><td>Not capable of "frequency hopping".</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>l.</td><td>Having data processing sub-systems and having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>'Automatic target tracking' providing, at any antenna rotation, the predicted target position beyond the time of the next antenna beam passage; or<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>6A008.l.1. does not control conflict alert capability in ATC systems, or 'marine radar'.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Note:  For the purposes of 6A008.l.1., 'automatic target tracking' is a processing technique that automatically determines and provides as output an extrapolated value of the most probable position of the target in real time. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Not used;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>Not used;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>4.</td><td>Configured to provide superposition and correlation, or fusion, of target data within six seconds from two or more 'geographically dispersed' radar sensors to improve the aggregate performance beyond that of any single sensor specified in 6A008.f. or 6A008.i. Technical Note:  For the purposes of 6A008.l.4., sensors are considered 'geographically dispersed' when each location is distant from any other more than 1 500 m in any direction. Mobile sensors are always considered 'geographically dispersed'. <table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>See also Military Goods Controls.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>6A008.l.4. does not control systems, equipment and assemblies designed for 'vessel traffic service'.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Notes:  For the purposes of 6A008: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>'Marine radar' is a radar that is designed to navigate safely at sea, inland waterways or near-shore environments.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>'Vessel traffic service' is a vessel traffic monitoring and control service similar to air traffic control for "aircraft".</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

|  | 6A102 | Radiation hardened 'detectors', other than those specified in 6A002, specially designed or modified for protecting against nuclear effects (e.g. electromagnetic pulse (EMP), X-rays, combined blast and thermal effects) and usable for "missiles", designed or rated to withstand radiation levels which meet or exceed a total irradiation dose of 5 × 105 rads (silicon). Technical Note: In 6A102, a 'detector' is defined as a mechanical, electrical, optical or chemical device that automatically identifies and records, or registers a stimulus such as an environmental change in pressure or temperature, an electrical or electromagnetic signal or radiation from a radioactive material. This includes devices that sense by one time operation or failure. |
| --- | --- | --- |

</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>7A003. does not apply to 'inertial measurement equipment or systems' which are certified for use on "civil aircraft" by civil aviation authorities of one or more EU Member States or Wassenaar Arrangement Participating States.</td></tr>

 Technical Notes:  For the purposes of 7A003: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>'Inertial measurement equipment or systems' incorporate accelerometers or gyroscopes to measure changes in velocity and orientation in order to determine or maintain heading or position without requiring an external reference once aligned. 'Inertial measurement equipment or systems' include:<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Attitude and Heading Reference Systems (AHRSs);</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Gyrocompasses;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Inertial Measurement Units (IMUs);</td></tr>

</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Inertial Navigation Systems (INSs);</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Inertial Reference Systems (IRSs);</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>—</td><td>Inertial Reference Units (IRUs).</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>'Positional aiding references' independently provide position, and include:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td> "Satellite navigation system";</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td> "Data-Based Referenced Navigation" ("DBRN").</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>Designed for "aircraft", land vehicles or vessels, providing position without the use of 'positional aiding references', and having any of the following "accuracies" subsequent to normal alignment:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>0,8 nautical miles per hour (nm/hr) "Circular Error Probable" ("CEP") rate or less (better);</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>0,5 % distanced travelled "CEP" or less (better); or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>Total drift of 1 nautical mile "CEP" or less (better) in a 24 hr period;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Note:  For the purposes of 7A003.a.1., 7A003.a.2. and 7A003.a.3., the performance parameters typically apply to 'inertial measurement equipment or systems' designed for "aircraft", vehicles and vessels, respectively. These parameters result from the utilisation of specialised non-'positional aiding references' (e.g., altimeter, odometer, velocity log). As a consequence, the specified performance values cannot be readily converted between these parameters. Equipment designed for multiple platforms are evaluated against each applicable entry 7A003.a.1., 7A003.a.2., or 7A003.a.3. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Designed for "aircraft", land vehicles or vessels, with an embedded 'positional aiding reference' and providing position after loss of all 'positional aiding references' for a period of up to 4 minutes, having an "accuracy" of less (better) than 10 meters "CEP";<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>7A003.b. refers to systems in which 'inertial measurement equipment or systems' and other independent 'positional aiding references' are built into a single unit (i.e., embedded) in order to achieve improved performance.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>Designed for "aircraft", land vehicles or vessels, providing heading or True North determination and having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>A maximum operating angular rate less (lower) than 500 deg/s and a heading "accuracy" without the use of 'positional aiding references' equal to or less (better) than 0,07 deg sec(Lat) (equivalent to 6 arc minutes rms at 45 degrees latitude); or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>A maximum operating angular rate equal to or greater (higher) than 500 deg/s and a heading "accuracy" without the use of 'positional aiding references' equal to or less (better) than 0,2 deg sec(Lat) (equivalent to 17 arc minutes rms at 45 degrees latitude); or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>d.</td><td>Providing acceleration measurements or angular rate measurements, in more than one dimension, and having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Performance specified in 7A001 or 7A002 along any axis, without the use of any aiding references; or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Being "space-qualified" and providing angular rate measurements having an "angle random walk" along any axis of less (better) than or equal to 0,1 degree per square root hour.<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>7A003.d.2. does not control 'inertial measurement equipment or systems' that contain "spinning mass gyros" as the only type of gyro.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>7A004</td><td>'Star trackers' and components therefor, as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>SEE ALSO 7A104.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>'Star trackers' with a specified azimuth "accuracy" of equal to or less (better) than 20 seconds of arc throughout the specified lifetime of the equipment;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Components specially designed for equipment specified in 7A004.a. as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Optical heads or baffles;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Data processing units.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Note:  For the purposes of 7A004.a., 'star trackers' are also referred to as stellar attitude sensors or gyro-astro compasses. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>7A005</td><td>"Satellite navigation system" receiving equipment having any of the following and specially designed components therefor:<table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>SEE ALSO 7A105.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>For equipment specially designed for military use, SEE MILITARY GOODS CONTROLS.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>Employing a decryption algorithm specially designed or modified for government use to access the ranging code for position and time; or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Employing 'adaptive antenna systems'.<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>7A005.b. does not control "satellite navigation system" receiving equipment that only uses components designed to filter, switch, or combine signals from multiple omni-directional antennae that do not implement adaptive antenna techniques.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Note:  For the purposes of 7A005.b., 'adaptive antenna systems' dynamically generate one or more spatial nulls in an antenna array pattern by signal processing in the time domain or frequency domain. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>7A006</td><td>Airborne altimeters operating at frequencies other than 4,2 to 4,4 GHz inclusive and having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>SEE ALSO 7A106.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>'Power management'; or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Using phase shift key modulation. Technical Note:  For the purposes of 7A006.a., 'power management' is changing the transmitted power of the altimeter signal so that received power at the "aircraft" altitude is always at the minimum necessary to determine the altitude. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>7A008</td><td>Underwater sonar navigation systems using doppler velocity or correlation velocity logs integrated with a heading source and having a positioning "accuracy" of equal to or less (better) than 3 % of distance travelled "Circular Error Probable" ("CEP") and specially designed components therefor.<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>7A008 does not control systems specially designed for installation on surface vessels or systems requiring acoustic beacons or buoys to provide positioning data.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>N.B.</td><td>For acoustic systems, see 6A001.a., and for correlation-velocity and Doppler-velocity sonar log equipment, see 6A001.b.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 For other marine systems, see 8A002. </td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>7A101</td><td>Linear accelerometers, other than those specified in 7A001, designed for use in inertial navigation systems or in guidance systems of all types, usable in 'missiles', having all of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>A "bias""repeatability" of less (better) than 1 250 micro g; and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>A "scale factor""repeatability" of less (better) than 1 250 ppm;</td></tr>
</tbody>

<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>7A101 does not control accelerometers specially designed and developed as Measurement While Drilling (MWD) Sensors for use in downhole well service operations.</td></tr>

</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>In 7A101 the measurement of "bias" and "scale factor" refers to one sigma standard deviation with respect to a fixed calibration over a period of one year;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<tbody><tr><td></td><td>7A102</td><td>All types of gyros, other than those specified in 7A002, usable in 'missiles', with a rated "drift rate" 'stability' of less than 0,5° (1 sigma or rms) per hour in a 1 g environment and specially designed components therefor. Technical Notes: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>In 7A102 'missile' means complete rocket systems and unmanned aerial vehicle systems capable of a range exceeding 300 km.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>In 7A102 'stability' is defined as a measure of the ability of a specific mechanism or performance coefficient to remain invariant when continuously exposed to a fixed operating condition (IEEE STD 528-2001 paragraph 2.247).</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>

<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Accelerometers specified in 7A001.a.1. or 7A001.a.2., designed for use in inertial navigation systems or in guidance systems of all types, and usable in 'missiles';<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>7A103.a.2. does not control equipment containing accelerometers specified in 7A001.a.1. or 7A001.a.2. where such accelerometers are specially designed and developed as MWD (Measurement While Drilling) sensors for use in down-hole well services operations</td></tr>
</tbody>

</td></tr>

</td></tr>

</tbody>

</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>One or more external sensors used to update the position and/or velocity, either periodically or continuously throughout the flight (e.g., satellite navigation receiver, radar altimeter, and/or Doppler radar);and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>c.</td><td>Integration hardware and software;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>In 7A103.c. 'CEP' (Circular Error Probable or Circle of Equal Probability) is a measure of accuracy, defined as the radius of the circle inside of which there is a 50 % probability of being located.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>d.</td><td>Three axis magnetic heading sensors, designed or modified to be integrated with flight control and navigation systems, other than those specified in 6A006, having all of the following characteristics, and specially designed components therefor:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Internal tilt compensation in pitch (± 90 degrees) and roll (± 180 degrees) axes; and</td></tr>

 Technical Note:  In 7A103 'missile' means complete rocket systems and unmanned aerial vehicle systems capable of a range exceeding 300 km. </td></tr>

</td></tr>

</tbody>

</td></tr>

</td></tr>
</tbody>

</tbody>

<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>'Active noise reduction or cancellation systems' or magnetic bearings, specially designed for power transmission systems; Technical Note:  For the purposes of 8A002.o.3.b., 'active noise reduction or cancellation systems' incorporate electronic control systems capable of actively reducing equipment vibration by the generation of anti-noise or anti-vibration signals directly to the source. </td></tr>

</table>
</td></tr>

<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>"UAVs" or unmanned "airships", designed to have controlled flight out of the direct 'natural vision' of the 'operator' and having any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Having all of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>A maximum 'endurance' greater than or equal to 30 minutes but less than 1 hour; and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Designed to take-off and have stable controlled flight in wind gusts equal to or exceeding 46,3 km/h (25 knots); or</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>A maximum 'endurance' of 1 hour or greater;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Notes:  For the purposes of 9A012.a.: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>'Operator' is a person who initiates or commands the "UAV" or unmanned "airship" flight.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>'Endurance' is to be calculated for ISA conditions (ISO 2533:1975) at sea level in zero wind.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>'Natural vision'means unaided human sight, with or without corrective lenses.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Related equipment and components, as follows:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Not used;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Not used;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>Equipment or components, specially designed to convert a manned "aircraft" or manned "airship", to a "UAV" or unmanned "airship", specified in 9A012.a.;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>4.</td><td>Air breathing reciprocating or rotary internal combustion type engines, specially designed or modified to propel "UAVs" or unmanned "airships", at altitudes above 15 240 metres (50 000 feet).</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>9A101</td><td>Turbojet and turbofan engines, other than those specified in 9A001, as follows;<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>Engines having all of the following characteristics:<table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>'Maximum thrust value' greater than 400 N excluding civil certified engines with a 'maximum thrust value' greater than 8 890 N;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Specific fuel consumption of 0,15 kg N-1 h-1 or less;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>'Dry weight' less than 750 kg; and</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>4.</td><td>'First-stage rotor diameter' less than 1 m;</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Notes: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>For the purpose of 9A101.a.1., 'maximum thrust value' is the manufacturer’s demonstrated maximum thrust for the engine type un-installed at sea level static conditions using the ICAO standard atmosphere. The civil type certified thrust value will be equal to or less than the manufacturer’s demonstrated maximum thrust for the engine type un-installed.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>Specific fuel consumption is determined at maximum continuous thrust for engine type un-installed at sea level static conditions using the ICAO standard atmosphere.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>'Dry weight' is the weight of the engine without fluids (fuel, hydraulic fluid, oil, etc.) and does not include the nacelle (housing).</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>4.</td><td>'First-stage rotor diameter' is the diameter of the first rotating stage of the engine, whether a fan or compressor, measured at the leading edge of the blade tips.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Engines designed or modified for use in "missiles" or unmanned aerial vehicles specified in 9A012 or 9A112.a.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<table>
<tbody><tr><td></td><td>9A102</td><td>'Turboprop engine systems' specially designed for unmanned aerial vehicles specified in 9A012 or 9A112.a., and specially designed components therefor, having a 'maximum power' greater than 10 kW.<table><tbody><tr><td>Note:</td><td>9A102 does not control civil certified engines.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Notes: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>For the purposes of 9A102, a 'turboprop engine system' incorporates all of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>Turboshaft engine;and</td></tr>

</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>Power transmission system to transfer the power to a propeller.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>For the purposes of 9A102, the 'maximum power' is achieved un-installed at sea level static conditions using ICAO standard atmosphere.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

 Technical Notes: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>For the purposes of 9E003.a.2.a., 'thermally decoupled liners' are liners that feature at least a support structure designed to carry mechanical loads and a combustion facing structure designed to protect the support structure from the heat of combustion. The combustion facing structure and support structure have independent thermal displacement (mechanical displacement due to thermal load) with respect to one another, i.e., they are thermally decoupled.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>For the purposes of 9E003.a.2.a. and 9E003.a.2.d., 'combustor exit temperature' is the bulk average gas path total (stagnation) temperature between the combustor exit plane and the leading edge of the turbine inlet guide vane (i.e., measured at engine station T40 as defined in SAE ARP 755A) when the engine is running in a "steady state mode" of operation at the certificated maximum continuous operating temperature.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>For the purposes of 9.E.3.a.2.e., 'pressure gain combustion' refers to a combustor where the bulk average stagnation pressure at the combustor outlet is greater than the bulk average stagnation pressure at the combustor inlet due primarily to the combustion process, when the engine is running in a "steady state mode" of operation.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>b.</td><td>'Hole shape ratio' greater than 5,65; and</td></tr>

</tbody>
</table>
 Technical Notes:  For the purposes of 9E003.c.: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>The 'cross-sectional area' is the area of the hole in the plane perpendicular to the hole axis.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>'Hole shape ratio' is the nominal length of the axis of the hole divided by the square root of its minimum 'cross-sectional area'.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>'Incidence angle' is the acute angle measured between the plane tangential to the aerofoil surface and the hole axis at the point where the hole axis enters the aerofoil surface.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>4.</td><td>Methods for manufacturing holes include "laser" beam machining, water jet machining, Electro-Chemical Machining (ECM) or Electrical Discharge Machining (EDM).</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</td></tr>

</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>Width:</td><td>The largest of any of the following:<table><tbody><tr><td>a.</td><td>The outside dimension from valve cover to valve cover;</td></tr>

</table>
</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

 Technical Notes:  For the purposes of 9E003.k.: <table><tbody><tr><td>1.</td><td>Propulsion inlet systems include core flow pre-coolers.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>2.</td><td>'Reheat systems' provide additional thrust by combusting fuel in exhaust and/or bypass flow downstream of the last turbomachinery stage. 'Reheat systems' are also referred to as afterburners.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>3.</td><td>'Active thermal management systems' employ methods other than passive oil-to-air cooling or oil-to-fuel cooling, such as vapour cycle systems.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>4.</td><td>'Compression system' is any stage or combination of stages between the engine inlet face and the combustor that increases gas path pressure through mechanical work.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<table><tbody><tr><td>5.</td><td>An 'engine rotor support' is the bearing supporting the main engine shaft that drives the compression system or turbine rotors.</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
